Source positions are staged through shared memory in\n// tiles of BLOCK_SIZE bodies. Each thread still visits j = 0..n-1 in order, so\n// the floating-point accumulation order matches the sequential reference.\n// Only pos is read and only vel is written, so there is no cross-thread hazard.\n__global__ void computeForcesKernel(Body* bodies, int n) {\n    __shared__ double tileX[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double tileY[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double tileZ[BLOCK_SIZE];\n\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    const bool active = i < n;\n    const double xi = active ? bodies[i].pos.x : 0.0;\n    const double yi = active ? bodies[i].pos.y : 0.0;\n    const double zi = active ? bodies[i].pos.z : 0.0;\n\n    double Fx = 0.0, Fy = 0.0, Fz = 0.0;\n\n    for (int tile = 0; tile < n; tile += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int j = tile + threadIdx.x;\n        if (j < n) {\n            tileX[threadIdx.x] = bodies[j].pos.x;\n            tileY[threadIdx.x] = bodies[j].pos.y;\n            tileZ[threadIdx.x] = bodies[j].pos.z;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        const int count = min(BLOCK_SIZE, n - tile);\n        for (int k = 0; k < count; ++k) {\n            const double dx = tileX[k] - xi;\n            const double dy = tileY[k] - yi;\n            const double dz = tileZ[k] - zi;\n            const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n            const double invDist = 1.0 / sqrt(distSqr);\n            const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n            Fx += dx * invDist3;\n            Fy += dy * invDist3;\n            Fz += dz * invDist3;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n\n    if (active) {\n        bodies[i].vel.x += DT * Fx;\n        bodies[i].vel.y += DT * Fy;\n        bodies[i].vel.z += DT * Fz;\n    }\n}\n\n__global__ void integrateBodiesKernel(Body* bodies, int n) {\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (i < n) {\n        bodies[i].pos.x += bodies[i].vel.x * DT;\n        bodies[i].pos.y += bodies[i].vel.y * DT;\n        bodies[i].pos.z += bodies[i].vel.z * DT;\n    }\n}"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":772,"cache_read_input_tokens":38085,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":772},"output_tokens":16,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"31bfef7f-170d-43fd-8400-7569164c063a","uuid":"2f0eb9eb-c793-4874-a65a-a60eb3c1ca3c","timestamp":"2026-10-12T13:04:35.174Z","request_id":"req_011CfxPbxGAT7wAcregcCie6"}

Source positions are staged through shared memory in\n// tiles of BLOCK_SIZE bodies. Each thread still visits j = 0..n-1 in order, so\n// the floating-point accumulation order matches the sequential reference.\n// Only pos is read and only vel is written, so there is no cross-thread hazard.\n__global__ void computeForcesKernel(Body* bodies, int n) {\n    __shared__ double tileX[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double tileY[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double tileZ[BLOCK_SIZE];\n\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    const bool active = i < n;\n    const double xi = active ? bodies[i].pos.x : 0.0;\n    const double yi = active ? bodies[i].pos.y : 0.0;\n    const double zi = active ? bodies[i].pos.z : 0.0;\n\n    double Fx = 0.0, Fy = 0.0, Fz = 0.0;\n\n    for (int tile = 0; tile < n; tile += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int j = tile + threadIdx.x;\n tileY[k] - yi;\n            const double dz = tileZ[k] - zi;\n            const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n            const double invDist = 1.0 / sqrt(distSqr);\n            const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n            Fx += dx * invDist3;\n            Fy += dy * invDist3;\n            Fz += dz * invDist3;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n\n    if (active) {\n        bodies[i].vel.x += DT * Fx;\n        bodies[i].vel.y += DT * Fy;\n        bodies[i].vel.z += DT * Fz;\n    }\n}\n\n__global__ void integrateBodiesKernel(Body* bodies, int n) {\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (i < n) {\n        bodies[i].pos.x += bodies[i].vel.x * DT;\n        bodies[i].pos.y += bodies[i].vel.y * DT;\n        bodies[i].pos.z += bodies[i].vel.z * DT;\n    }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const std::vector<Body>& bodies) {\n    double energy = 0.0;\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    \n    // Kinetic energy (assuming unit mass)\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        energy += 0.5 * (body.vel.x * body.vel.x + \n                        body.vel.y * body.vel.y + \n                        body.vel.z * body.vel.z);\n    }\n    \n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j bodies[j].pos.z - bodies[i].pos.z;\n            const double dist = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            energy -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const std::vector<Body>& bodies) {\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        // Check for NaN or Inf values\n        if (!std::isfinite(body.pos.x) || !std::isfinite(body.pos.y) || !std::isfinite(body.pos.z) ||\n            !std::isfinite(body.vel.x) || !std::isfinite(body.vel.y) || Source positions are staged through shared memory in\n// tiles of BLOCK_SIZE bodies. Each thread still visits j = 0..n-1 in order, so\n// the floating-point accumulation order matches the sequential reference.\n// Only pos is read and only vel is written, so there is no cross-thread const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    const bool active = i < n;\n    const double xi = active ? bodies[i].pos.x : 0.0;\n    const double yi = active ? bodies[i].pos.y : 0.0;\n    const double zi = active ? bodies[i].pos.z : 0.0;\n\n    double Fx = 0.0, Fy = 0.0, Fz = 0.0;\n\n    for (int tile = 0; tile < n; tile += BLOCK_SIZE) {\n        const int j = tile + threadIdx.x;\n tileY[k] - yi;\n            const double dz = tileZ[k] - zi;\n            const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n            const double invDist = 1.0 / sqrt(distSqr);\n            const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n            Fx += dx * invDist3;\n            Fy += dy * invDist3;\n            Fz += dz * invDist3;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n\n    if (active) {\n        bodies[i].vel.x += DT * Fx;\n        bodies[i].vel.y += DT * Fy;\n        bodies[i].vel.z += DT * Fz;\n    }\n}\n\n__global__ void integrateBodiesKernel(Body* bodies, int n) {\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (i < n) {\n        bodies[i].pos.x += bodies[i].vel.x * DT;\n        bodies[i].pos.y += bodies.size();\n    \n    // Kinetic energy (assuming unit mass)\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        energy += 0.5 * (body.vel.x * body.vel.x + \n                        body.vel.y * body.vel.y + \n                        body.vel.z * body.vel.z);\n    }\n    \n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j bodies[j].pos.z - bodies[i].pos.z;\n            const double dist = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            energy -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const std::vector<Body>& bodies) {\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        // Check for std::abs(body.pos.z) > maxPos) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body position exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(body.vel.x) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.y) > maxVel || std::abs(body.vel.z) > maxVel) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011CfxQ7KuyNYgsfptKMoxYu","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The N-body benchmark now runs its force and integration steps as CUDA kernels on the GPU. The executable is still `nbody`, and only `nbody/nbody.cpp` and `nbody/CMakeLists.txt` were changed. No files were created, and the build was done out of tree in `/tmp`.\n\n**What changed**\n- **`nbody.cpp`**: `computeForces` and `integrateBodies` are now `__global__` kernels. Bodies stay on the device for all steps and are copied back once for the results and validation. The timed region covers only the kernel launches and the final sync, as the original timed only the simulation loop.\n- **Force kernel**: one thread per body. Positions are staged through shared memory in tiles. Each thread still visits j = 0..n-1 in the original order, so the summation order is unchanged. Only `pos` is read and only `vel` is written, so there are no cross-thread races.\n- **Block size**: 64 threads. I swept 32 to 512 on the RTX 3090. 64 was best or near-best across n=1024 to 16384, and the hash was identical for every block size.\n- **`CMakeLists.txt`**: enables the CUDA language and compiles `nbody.cpp` as CUDA with `set_source_files_properties`. The architecture defaults to `native`. `cmake_minimum_required` is raised to 3.24 for that. Host flags (`-O3 -march=native -Wall -Wextra`) pass through `-Xcompiler`.\n\n**Results** (default FMA mode, same `-s 10`)\n\n| n | CPU (original) | GPU |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1024 | 77 ms | 7 ms |\n| 4096 | 1239 ms | 29 ms |\n| 16384 | ~19.9 s | ~329 ms |\n\n- Validation passes (`-v`), and n=0, 1, 2, 63, 64, and 65 all run cleanly.\n- Results match the CPU within about 1e-12 relative. Hashes differ from the CPU build, though.\n\n**Floating-point note**\n- The GPU's default FMA contraction changes the last bits compared with the original. The original's own hash also changes depending on FMA contraction: the project's CMake build gives `1eed6713692b7ce8` at n=1024, while a strict no-contraction CPU build gives `37bf5bbdacd6cae0`.\n- The GPU build with `--fmad=false` reproduces the strict-IEEE hash exactly. I left FMA on, because it costs about 20% in speed.\n- If you need hashes to match the strict-IEEE reference, add `--fmad=false` to the CUDA compile options in `CMakeLists.txt`. That's a one-line change.\n\n**Trade-offs and limits**\n- Each body's sum runs in one thread to preserve the summation order. That means only n threads exist, so small n (for example 1024) doesn't fill the GPU. Splitting the j-loop across threads would scale better at small n, but it would change the summation order.\n- The RTX 3090 has weak FP64 throughput, which bounds large-n performance. I kept double precision because the output and hash depend on it.\n- compute-sanitizer could not load its injection library here, so I did not get a memcheck or racecheck run. The race-freedom argument rests on the tile loads and syncs."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":487,"cache_read_input_tokens":63349,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":487},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"31bfef7f-170d-43fd-8400-7569164c063a","uuid":"64f5fde8-5046-4b6b-9fb3-1191b8e124b4","timestamp":"2026-10-12T13:11:14.197Z","request_id":"req_011CfxQ7KdMiYvg3LGhYusdp"}
